Tattva Sūtra (‘Aphorisms on the Supreme Truth’) was originally composed by Bharadvāja Muni and commented upon by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura in 1894 and serialised in his Sajjana Toṣaṇī journal in 1895. The Tattva Sūtra is comprised of five chapters containing fifty Sanskrit sūtras.

# Tattva Sutra

## Introduction

Tattva Sūtra (‘Aphorisms on the Supreme Truth’) was originally composed by Bharadvāja Muni and commented upon by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura in 1894 and serialised in his *Sajjana Toṣaṇī* journal in 1895. The *Tattva* *Sūtra* is comprised of five chapters containing fifty Sanskrit *sūtras*. These five chapters are as follows:

- The Subject of Reality.
- The Subject of Consciousness.
- The Subject of Non-consciousness.
- The Subject Concerning the Relationship Between the Two.
- The Subject Concerning the Conclusion.

The Ṭhākura writes of the origin of *Tattva Sūtras* in his *Navadvīpa Bhāva Taraṅga*:

*bharadvāja-ṭīlā tathā dekhite sundara*  
*gaura bhaji’ yathā bharadvāja muni-vara*  
*labhiyā caitanya-prema sūtra prakāśila*  
*kata-śata bahir-mukha jane bhakti dila*

**Here is Bharadvāja Ṭīlā, which is beautiful to see. Here, Bharadvāja, the best of munis, worshipped Gaura and achieved** ***caitanya*****–*****prema*****. Bharadvāja wrote** ***sūtras*****, and bestowed** ***bhakti*** **to thousands of persons who were averse to the Lord. (*****Navadvīpa Bhāva Taraṅga*** **21)**

At the conclusion of this work, Bhaktivinoda states that a commentary to Bharadvāja’s *Tattva Sūtras* was commented upon in Sanskrit by the Vaiṣṇava scholar, Śrī Gopīnātha Paṇḍita. From the structure of the *Tattva Sūtras*, it seems that the Sanskrit commentary preceding Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s Bengali commentary is that of Gopīnātha Paṇḍita. We have taken the liberty of separating these commentaries for clarity.

## Chapter 1. Tattva Prakaraṇam (The Chapter Concerning the Supreme Reality)

## Introduction by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura

*praṇamya kṛṣṇa caitanyaṁ bhāradvājaṁ sanātanam*  
*tattva sūtraṁ sa vyākhyānāṁ bhāṣāyāṁ vivṛtaṁ mayā*

**Offering respects unto Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya and the Sage Bhāradvāja, I now present the eternal** ***Tattva Sūtras*** **with an explanation and my commentary.**

This *Tattva Sūtra* is without beginning and is to be experienced directly, thus it is considered to be the essence of all the *Vedas*. It stems from Bhāradvāja and Śrī Caitanya, therefore, it is the foundation of all *sat*–*śāstra* (transcendental literature). In this text, only the Absolute Truth is accepted.

Thus, in the First Canto of the *Bhāgavata*, Sūta says:

*vadanti tat tattva-vidas tattvaṁ yaj jñānam advayam*  
*brahmeti paramātmeti bhagavān iti śabdyate*

**The seers that know the Absolute Truth call this non-dual substance Brahman, Paramātmā or Bhagavān. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **1. 2.11)**

In the seventh *mantra* of the *Yajur Veda’s Vājasaneya-saṁhitopaniṣad* it is said:

*yasmin sarvāṇi bhūtāni ātmaivābhūd vijānataḥ*  
*tatra ko mohaḥ kaḥ śoka ekatvam anupaśyataḥ*

**At that time, when one sees oneness between all beings and the Paramātmā, how can there be illusion or misery for that wise man who sees with equal vision?**

Then in the *Gītopaniṣad*, Bhagavān states:

*mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat kiñcid asti dhanañjaya*  
*mayi sarvam idaṁ protaṁ sūtre maṇi-gaṇā iva*

**Dhanañjaya, there is nothing superior to Me. All things rest upon Me, just like jewels strung upon a thread. (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **7.7)**

In the *maṅgalācaraṇa* of the *Nārada Pañcarātra*, the author of the text says:

*dhyāyet taṁ paramaṁ brahma paramātmānam īśvaram*  
*nirīham ati-nirliptaṁ nirguṇaṁ prakṛteḥ param*  
*sarveśaṁ sarva-rūpaṁ ca sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇam*  
*satyaṁ nityaṁ ca puruṣaṁ purāṇaṁ param avyayam*

**One should meditate on that Supreme, who is Brahman, Paramātmā and Īśvara. He is desireless, completely pure, beyond the modes of nature and transcends the material world. He is the Lord of all things, He manifests all forms, and He is the cause of all causes. He is the Supreme Truth, eternal, the primeval Personality, Supreme and imperishable. (*****Nārada Pañcarātra*** **1.4)**

In the Fourth Chapter of the *Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa*, it is written:

*yasmād anutaraṁ nāsti yasmān nāsti bṛhattaram*  
*yena viśvam idaṁ vyāptam ajena jagad-ādinā*

**There is nothing as minute as Him, and nothing that is greater than Him. That unborn creator of the world pervades the entire universe. (*****Mārkandeya Purāṇa*** **4.38)**

With the establishment of the non-duality of the Absolute Reality, a doubt arises. In other words, in relation to that Reality itself as being all substances (*padārtha*). If that is so, then there is no necessity to imagine any other substance as separate from that. To resolve this doubt, the term *tattva* (reality) is used to denote that which is *para* (Supreme). It should be considered that in this context, the author of the *sūtras* has only referred to the Supreme Being as *tattva*. *Cit* (consciousness) and *acit* (unconscious) have been referred to as *padārthas*. Indeed, *cit* and *acit* are counted amongst the *padārthas* of this visible world. Those things in relation to Bhagavān, being incomprehensible, cannot be defined as *padārtha*. If a word conveys some meaning when mentioned, then that word can be called a *pada*, and the object indicated by a *pada* is called a *padārtha*. Those things in relation to Bhagavān transcend logic, therefore, the *śruti* states:

*yato vāco nivartante aprāpya manasā saha*

**He is beyond the scope of speech and thought. (*****Taittirīya Upaniṣad*** **2.4.1)**

If this is applied, then Bhagāvan is referred to by the term *tattva*, and not by the term *padārtha*. Bhagāvan is different from *padārthas*, but *padārthas* can never exist separately from Bhagāvan. This topic is to be experienced directly, however it is not subject to logical analysis.

Therefore, on the subject of Reality, (*tattva-prakaraṇa*) the author of the *sūtras* has established the first *sūtra* thus:

## Sutra 1

*ekaḥ paro’nanyaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme is One without a second**

**Комментарий:**

*eka evādvitīyaḥ parameśvaraḥ tad-anyaḥ ko’pi paro nāstīty arthaḥ ‘ekam evātṛtīyaṁ neha nānāsti kiñcanenti’ śruteḥ*

**The Supreme Lord is one and indivisible, and there is no other entity equal to or superior to Him anywhere – “Before the creation of this universe, there existed only one non-dual, Absolute Reality” (*****Chāndogya Upaniṣad*** **6.2.1) and – “This perceptible universe is non-different from Brahman.”(*****Bṛhad-Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad*** **4.4.19)**

***nanu athāto brahma-jijñāsā, athāto dharma-jijñāsā iti vyāsādi-sūtrakarair atha-śabdas tu maṅgala-sūcakasya tat-taj-jijñāsā-padasya tat-tad-viṣayaka-jñānecchā puruṣeṇa kartavyeti puruṣecchā kṛtādhīna-jñāna-viṣayī-bhūta-dharma-brahma-rūpa-śāstra-pratipādya-vastu-sūcakasya copanyāsena maṅgalācaraṇaṁ viṣayādi-sūcana-rūpaṁ pratijñāṁ ca kṛtvā śāstram ārabdham. tattva-sūtra-kāreṇa tu tad akṛtvā kathaṁ śāstram udakrāntam iti cet, na, asmin śāstre prathamataḥ sūtre parama-maṅgala-svarūpa-parameśvara-tattva-nirūpaṇa-prastāvena pṛthak maṅgalācaraṇasyānavaśyakatvāt. etac-chāstra-pratipādya-prayojanībhūta-vastunaḥ sva-prakāśatvena svataḥ-siddha-pratyaya-gocaratayā ca puruṣecchā kṛtādhīna-jñāna-viṣayatvābhāvāt tad-arthaṁ jijñāsā kartavyeti viṣaya-sūcanā-dvārā pratijñāyā apy anucitatvāt tad anādṛtya prathamataḥ sūtram aracayeti*****.**

Authors of *sūtra*-texts such as Vyāsa who have written *Brahma-sūtra, Dharma-sūtra* etc., began their works with *athāto brahma jijñāsā* (‘now, let us inquire into Brahman’) and *athāto dharma jijñāsā* (‘now, let us inquire into *dharma’*). By employing the auspicious word *atha* (‘now’) they framed the initial *sūtras* with the intent of addressing the questions of a person who seeks knowledge about the principles of these *śāstras*. All the *śāstra* concerning knowledge of *dharma-tattva* and *brahma-tattva*, which are based on the pursuit of knowledge by such inquisitive persons, begin with a *maṅgalācaraṇa* (auspicious invocation) or introductory statements that outline the subject matter. Is it not necessary for the author of the *Tattva Sūtra* to also follow the same practice? What is the reason for the commencement of this *śāstra* without the usual *maṅgalācaraṇa* starting with *atha* etc?

Regarding the possibility of this question, the author states that in this *śāstra*, since the purpose is to establish the ultimate truth of the Supreme Lord, who is Himself the embodiment of auspiciousness, the absence of a separate *maṅgalācaraṇa* is indicated to be unnecessary. The subject-matter of this *śāstra* is that the ultimate attainment of life for the *jīva* is that Entity who is self-revelatory by nature, and is experienced through the self-evident realisation of a pure *ātmā*. Because the Supreme Truth is not subordinate to the wishful thinking of the inquirer, and since ordinary methods of inquiry and affirmation are considered to be inappropriate in dealing with such a subject-matter, the author of the *Tattva Sūtra* has disregarded conventional tradition and commenced the writing of this first *sūtra* in an innovative manner.

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

*sac-cid-ānanda-sāndrātmā sāra-grāhi-jana-priyaḥ*  
*dīna-kāruṇya-pūrābdhir jīyān madana-mohanaḥ*  
*tat-kṛpāmṛta-bindūnāṁ pipāsa-stokitāśayaḥ*  
*prācīna-tattva-sūtrāṇi vivṛṇomi yathā-mati*

**May Madana-mohana, who is of condensed eternity, cognisance and bliss, who is loved by those persons who seek that which is essential, who is an ocean of compassion to the fallen, always be victorious. Being eager to taste a drop of His mercy, I will comment upon this ancient** ***Tattva-sūtra*****, in accordance with my understanding.**

That one who is referred to as the Supreme Lord is the only Reality. No other substance can be recognised as the Ultimate Reality (*para-tattva*)**.**

## Sutra 2

*aguṇo’pi sarva-śaktir ameyatvāt*

**Перевод:**

**Being unlimited, He has no mundane qualities and possesses all potencies**

**Комментарий:**

***sa ca parameśvaraḥ aguṇo’pi guṇātīto’pi sarva-śaktimān, pratyakṣādi-laukikāgamatvād ity arthaḥ. ‘parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate*** ***svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca’*** ***iti śrutiḥ***

That Supreme Lord has no material attributes, is beyond the modes of material nature, and possesses all potencies. Due to His nature being beyond worldly evidence such as *pratyakṣa* (direct perception) etc**,** the *śruti* states, “Indeed, it is heard that the Supreme has various potencies such as His *jñāna-śakti, bala-śakti* and *kriyā-śakti*” (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.8).

***nanv ekasyādvitīyasya parameśvarasya sahāya-rāhityena viśva-sṛṣṭy-ādi-vividha-kārya-kartṛtvaṁ kathaṁ ghaṭata ity āśaṅkāṁ nirākaroti.***

How can the creation of the universe etc. be accomplished by that one, non-dual Supreme Lord without assistance? The doubt is addressed thus (in the next *sūtra*).

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

That Supreme Being is beyond qualities. Qualities are of two types – *prākṛta* (natural) and *aprākṛta* (transcendental). In regard to spiritual substances, some of the qualities that will be described later are all *aprākṛta*, in other words, they transcend the nature of *māyā*. All those qualities that are mentioned in regards to material substance are entirely *prākṛta*, i.e., they are within the scope of *māyā*. There is no necessity for a specific explanation of these two kinds of qualities here. In brief, the point is that the Supreme Truth transcends both types of qualities.

In this context, there is a concern about whether there can be any relation between that Reality who is beyond qualities and those substances that possess qualities. A detailed discussion with logic yields no satisfactory conclusion. Relation certainly depends upon inherent characteristics; this is directly observed in the perceptible world. A relationship between substances with opposite natures just as light and darkness can never be considered. However, the Supreme Lord is beyond all qualities and possesses all potencies. If this conclusion contradicts logic or there is a doubt, it is addressed here by stating that it is *aprameya* (unfathomable). What will be the observable evidence or comparison in the empirical world that can serve as proof of the Supreme Reality? Through *vyāpti-jñāna* (knowledge drawn from observing inherent properties), the presence of fire on a mountain is determined by observing smoke. It is stated in the commentary on the *Gautama Sūtra* by Vātsyāyana that, *meghonnatyā bhaviṣyati vṛṣṭir iti* (‘from the elevation of the clouds, the possibility of rain is inferred’). By this kind of reasoning, it is accepted that an inference about an unseen substance can be made based on the characteristics of visible substances.

However, since no characteristics of the Supreme Substance are observable in this world, such a type of inference is not applicable in relation to the Lord. *Liṅga darśanena apratyakṣorthānumīyate* (‘By observing characteristics, one may infer what cannot be seen.’) – this is the rule of *anumāna* (inference). However, inference in relation to those things connected to the Supreme is not like that. Realisation of the Supreme cannot be called ‘inferred.’ It is directly realised knowledge. This has been directly explained by Gautama as follows:

*indriyārtha-sannikṛṣotpannaṁ jñāna-vyapadeśyam avyabhicāri vyavasāyatmakaṁ pratyakṣaṁ,*  
*vātsyāyanakṛta bhāṣye indriyasyārthena sannikṛṣādutpāte yat jñānaṁ tat pratyakṣyaṁ*

The meaning is this – knowledge that arises from close proximity with an object of the senses is called *pratyakṣa*. The meaning of the word *sannikṛṣa* is ‘direct experience.’ If direct experience by the senses can be called *pratyakṣa,* then can there be any objection to calling direct experience of the conscious nature of the *ātmā* as *pratyakṣa* also? The senses are not the basis of knowledge; they can only be called the gateway to knowledge, and nothing more. Thus, if substances at the gateway are implied by *pratyakṣa*, then what is the fault in referring to substances outside the gateway as *pratyakṣa* also? On the contrary, that can certainly be expressed by the word *pratyakṣa*, and knowledge obtained through the senses can be considered as *anumāna* in relation to the *ātmā*. No sensory evidence is required to establish the existence of the inquirer, because it is self-evident. Similarly, Jagadīśvara, the Lord of the Universe, is realised through the function of *bhakti* which is self-evident. Thus, there is no necessity for inference based on characteristics in this regard. *Bhagavat-tattva* should not be analysed by logic based upon comparisons. Although that Supreme Truth who transcends the material modes and is the possessor of all potencies is extraordinary, nevertheless, He has been accepted and established through self-evident belief. Thus, it has been established that although the Supreme Being is beyond mundane attributes, due to His incalculability, He also possesses all potencies. Thus, in the Second Canto, Second chapter of the *Bhāgavata*, it is stated by Śukadeva:

*bhagavān sarva-bhūteṣu lakṣitaḥ svātmanā hariḥ*  
*dṛśyair buddhy-ādibhir draṣṭā lakṣaṇair anumāpakaiḥ*

**Bhagavān Hari, who witnesses all things and remains as the Internal Monitor within all beings, is perceived through sight, inference, intellect and other attributes. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **2.2.35)**

And also in the Fourth Canto, Twentieth Chapter:

*ekaḥ śuddhaḥ svayaṁ-jyotir nirguṇo ‘sau guṇāśrayaḥ*  
*sarva-go’nāvṛtaḥ sākṣī nirātmātmātmanaḥ paraḥ*

**He is One, pure, self-effulgent, transcendental to the modes of nature, the shelter of all good qualities, all-pervading, untouched by matter, the Supreme Witness, independent of other beings, and beyond all other** ***ātmās*****. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **4.20.7)**

Also it is stated in the *Nārada Pañcarātra*:

*prakṛteḥ param iṣṭaṁ ca sarveṣām abhivāñchitam*  
*svecchāmayaṁ paraṁ brahma pañca-rātrābhidhaṁ smṛtam*

**That Supreme Truth is transcendental to matter, loved and desired by all, supremely independent and understood through the** ***Pañcarātra*****. (*****Nārada Pañcarātra*** **2.1.2)**

By accepting the arguments and *śāstra* that were previously mentioned describing Jagadīśvara as being beyond attributes and possessing all potencies, the opposition could raise this kind of doubt –“If such contradictory conclusions are broadly accepted again and again, the truth will be distorted by irrational beliefs.” To resolve this doubt, the author of the *sūtras* states that harmonising contradictions in the Supreme Being is not unusual. Harmonising contradictions is not possible in material objects, but Supreme Reality does not relate to this world. If it is not supramundane, then how can its supreme nature be established?

## Sutra 3

*viruddha-sāmānyaṁ tasmin na citraṁ*

**Перевод:**

**It is not unusual that contradictory characteristics reside within Him**

**Комментарий:**

***tasmin parameśvare viruddha-dharmāṇāṁ sāhacaryaṁ na citraṁ nāścaryam ity arthaḥ. ‘apāṇi-pādo javano gṛhītā paśyaty acakṣuḥ sa śṛṇoty akarṇaḥ’iti śruteḥ***

In relation to the Supreme Lord, the coexistence of contradictory natures is not unusual or surprising. The *śruti* states, “He has no hands or feet, yet He grasps and moves. He sees without eyes and hears without ears.” (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 3.19)

***nanu nirguṇatve’pi sarva-śaktitvam iti kathaṁ viruddha-dharmāvasthitir iti śaṅkāṁ pariharati***

However, even without mundane attributes, how can the existence of contradictory attributes, such as being all-powerful, be accounted for? This doubt is resolved as follows.

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

Unlimited contradictory qualities are observed in the Lord. Anything that can be said about Him indicates contradiction. Stating that the Lord created is to acknowledge a modification in a Personality who is immutable. If one says that the Lord maintains, it attributes activity to a Personality who performs no activity. By stating that the Lord destroys, then inauspiciousness is seen in the most auspicious Personality. If one says that the Lord exists, then it asserts the boundary of time upon a Reality who is beyond time. There is no end to such contradictions. In reality, both words and the mind are inadequate for describing and contemplating the Lord. If all these topics are analysed through logic, there will never be a conclusion – rather, there is a likelihood of considerable inauspiciousness for the examiner.

Contemplating all these matters, sages like Cārvaka etc, have resorted to atheism. Many have become sceptical and have met with destruction. Thus, the sooner the mind withdraws from such harmful debates, the better. Belief in the faculty of *bhakti* is the only way to be saved from such inauspiciousness. The initial state of this belief can be called *śraddhā*; thus, *śraddhā* is fundamental. Therefore, we find in the Fourth Chapter of the *Gītā*:

*śraddhāvāl labhate jñānaṁ tat-paraḥ saṁyatendriyaḥ*  
*jñānaṁ labdhvā parāṁ śāntim acireṇādhigacchati*  
*ajñaś cāśraddadhānaś ca saṁśayātmā vinaśyati*  
*nāyaṁ loko’sti na paro na sukhaṁ saṁśayātmanaḥ*

**Those imbued with faith and who are devoted to controlling their senses attain this knowledge very swiftly. Thus they attain supreme peace. Those who are deluded, without faith and full of doubt are ruined. Such faithless people find no happiness in this world or the next. (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **4.39-40)**

Therefore, through self-evident belief, the harmony of all opposite qualities in Jagadīśvara must be accepted. If that does not occur, then the terrible result of atheism will arise. Such contradictions in the Lord are not unusual, as there is no reason to doubt them. A perceivable substance possesses a definite inherent nature.

At this point, determining the nature of the Supreme Lord is necessary.

## Sutra 4

*sa sac-cid-ānando jñānāgamyo bhakti-viṣayatvāt*

**Перевод:**

**He is of eternity, cognisance, and bliss, unknowable through** ***jñāna*****, and is the object of** ***bhakti*****.**

**Комментарий:**

***sa ca parameśvaraḥ satya-jñānānanda-maya-vigraho’vāṅ-mānasa-gocaro jñānenāgrāhaḥ kevalaṁ bhakti-grāhyatvāt. ‘yad vācā nabhyuditaṁ yan manasa na manute’ – iti śruteḥ, ‘bhaktyāham ekayā grāhyaḥ’ – iti smṛteḥ***

The Supreme Lord, is endowed with a form of *sac-cid-ānanda* (eternity, cognisance, and bliss); beyond the reach of speech and mind, he is not achieved through *jñāna* alone, but is accessible only through *bhakti*. The *śruti* states, “The mind cannot comprehend Him and words cannot describe Him.” (*Kena Upaniṣad* 1.4) The *smṛti* says, “I am understood only through *bhakti*.”(*Śrīmad Bhāgavatam* 11.12.21)

***nanu parameśvarasya bhakti-grāhyatve tattve grāhya-jagad-aṇḍaḥ pātitvaṁ syād ity āśaṅkā-nirasanāya pañcama-sūtram ārabhate***

Now, to address the concern that if the Supreme Lord is accessible only through *bhakti*, the perceptible universe might be considered corrupt, the fifth *sūtra* begins by refuting this doubt.

## Sutra 5

*sa ca satyo nityo’nādir ananto deśa-kālāparicchedāt*

**Перевод:**

**(He is reality, eternal, without beginning, infinite and beyond the limitations of space and time)**

**Комментарий:**

***sa parameśvaraḥ satyaḥ, asataḥ sattā-pradatvāt, ‘satyaṁ jñānam ānandaṁ brahma’ iti śruteḥ, nityo ‘vināśī vāre’yam ātmā’ iti śruteḥ. anādir ananta ‘ādy-anta-śūnyaḥ daiśika-kālikobhaya-pariccheda-śūnyatvāt ‘sa-bhūmiṁ sarvataḥ spṛṣṭa-atyatiṣṭhat’ iti śruteḥ. ‘sarvam āvṛtya tiṣṭhati’ iti smṛteś ca.***

That Supreme Lord is reality, for he imparts reality to that which is unreal. The *śruti* states, “The Supreme is truth, knowledge and bliss.” (*Taittirīya Upanisad* 2.1.2). He is eternal, as the *śruti* says – ‘That Supreme Ātmā is indestructible.’ (*Bṛhad āraṇyaka Upanisad* 4.5.14). He is without beginning and infinite as the *śruti* states, ‘He has no beginning or end. He is not limited by space and time.’ *Śruti* also states, ‘He exists, pervading everything in the world’(*Ṛg Veda* 10.9). And the *smṛti* says, “Thus He exists, pervading all things” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 13.14).

## Sutra 6

*paro’pi cij-jaḍābhyāṁ vilāsī viśva-siddheḥ*

**Перевод:**

**(Although transcendental, due to His being the Enjoyer of Pastimes, He manifests the universes consisting of both spirit and matter)**

**Комментарий:**

***cij-jaḍābhyāṁ prakṛti-puruṣābhyāṁ paro’pi bhagavān prakṛti-puruṣa-sambandhātmaka-viśva-sṛṣṭer hetor vilāsī vividha-vilāso bhagavān bhavatīty arthaḥ. ‘sa aikṣata eko’haṁ bahu syāṁ prajāyeyeti,’ ‘yato vā imāni bhūtāni jāyante’ ity ādi-śruteś ca.***

The meaning of this is that even though Bhagavān is beyond the both *prakṛti* (material nature) and *puruṣa* (the individual *jīvātmā*), He is the cause of the creation of the universe, which is characterised by the relationship between *prakṛti* and *puruṣa*. Being the Enjoyer, Bhagavān manifests various pastimes. Thus, the *śruti* states, “He glanced over material nature and said, ‘May I become many; let there be progeny’” (*Chāndogya Upaniṣad* 6.2.3 ) and ‘From Him all beings are born’ (*Taittirīya Upanisad* 3.1.1).

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

By exercising His own eternal potency, the Supreme Lord creates both conscious and non-conscious substances, and enjoys them. In this universe, so much wonderful proficiency is observed, so many pleasant arrangements can be seen, and so much design and harmony is continuously detected. If creation were carried out by an inert agent or by a lifeless consciousness, such diversity could not be observed. Can such unique activities as the inconceivable relationships of all objects with the senses, the arrangement of matter according to physical needs, the prosperity of human habitation due to the division of land and water, the beauty and functionality of the solar system due to the functions of planets, stars, and constellations, the determination of time by the regulation of seasons, and the fulfilment of bodily needs by the organs of the human body, arise from lifeless consciousness? If one does not acknowledge the enjoying nature of the Supreme Lord, one cannot come to a satisfactory conclusion. The *Kaṭhopaniṣad* says:

*yad idaṁ kiñca jagat sarvaṁ prāṇa ejati niḥsītam*  
*mahad-bhayaṁ vajram-udyataṁ ya etad vidur-amṛtāste bhavanti*  
*bhayād asyāgnis-tapati bhayāt tapati sūryaḥ*  
*bhayād indraś ca vāyuś ca mṛtyur dhāvati pañcamaḥ*

**He is the life-force that moves the whole universe. He is terrifying like a raised thunderbolt. Those that know Him achieve immortality. Out of fear of Him, fire burns. Out of fear of Him the sun blazes. Out of fear of Him Indra, Vāyu and Death run to perform their respective duties. (*****Kaṭhopaniṣad*** **2.3.2-3)**

In the Twenty-fifth Chapter of the Third Canto of the *Bhāgavata* it is stated:

*mad-bhayād vāti vāto ‘yaṁ sūryas tapati mad-bhayāt*  
*varṣatīndro dahaty agnir mṛtyuś carati mad-bhayāt*

**Out of fear of Me, the wind blows. Out of fear of Me, the sun emanates heat. From of fear of Me, Indra distributes rain and fire burns. Out of fear of Me, death consumes. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **3.25.42)**

Also, in the Chapter Twenty-nine of the Tenth Canto of the *Bhāgavata* it says:

*bhagavān api tā rātṛīḥ śāradotphulla-mallikāḥ*  
*vīkṣya rantuṁ manaś cakre yoga-māyām upāśritaḥ*

**Observing those Autumn nights with their blossoming jasmine flowers, even Bhagavān decided to enjoy and resorted to His** ***yogamāyā*****. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **10.29.1)**

From all this evidence, it seems that some playful Personality has established inviolable rules for the welfare of the world. It seems that the Lord’s pastimes are of two types. The creation of the universe and the establishment of unbreakable injunctions for the management of the world are a kind of pastime for Him. Dry *jñānīs* can also perceive this kind of pastime to a certain extent. The *līlā* of Bhagavān in this created universe is another kind of pastime. The jīvas are Bhagavān’s companions in these pastimes. The *jīva*, having deviated from its own inherent nature due to a desire for enjoyment and under the influence of material association, perceives a corresponding appearance of Bhagavān in whatever state he attains. Boundless compassion for the *jīva* is the only reason for Bhagavān’s appearance. All these appearances are called *avatāras*. From the state of an invertebrate up to the state of human completeness, some great *ṛṣis* recognise eight *avatāras*, some recognise eighteen, and some recognise twenty-four *avatāras.* Ten *avatāras* is the most popular opinion of most learned *ṛṣis*. All those *ṛṣis* envision ten specific states of the *jīva*, from his initial state of subjugation to the end. First is the state of the invertebrate, second is the state of the crustacean, third is the state of the vertebrate. fourth is the elevated state of possessing a backbone – in other words, the state of a man-beast, fifth is the state of the dwarf-human, sixth is the state of an uncivilised human, seventh is the state of a civilised human, eighth is the state of knowledge, ninth is the state of greater knowledge, and tenth is the state of annihilation. According to these gradual historical states of the *jīva*, the ten *avatāras* – Matsya, Kūrma, Varāha, Narasiṁha, Vāmana, Paraśurāma, Rāma, Kṛṣṇa, Buddha, and Kalki – are observed as transcendental pastimes. The narrations of these divine pastimes have been described through the medium of *parokṣavāda* (indirect, esoteric explanations) in all the *Purāṇas*, especially in the *Śrīmad Bhāgavata*. By the grace of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, those who have understood this knowledge of the *avatāras* through detailed observation, and who are experts in *bhakti*, have realised *kṛṣṇa-tattva*, particularly the unparalleled sweetness of the pastimes in *vraja*. This is supported in the composition, *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛṭa*.

*madhura-madhuram etan maṅgalaṁ maṅgalānāṁ*  
*sakala-nigama-vallī-sat-phalaṁ cit-svarūpam*  
*sakṛd api parigītaṁ śraddhayā helayā vā*  
*bhṛgu-vara nara-mātraṁ tārayet kṛṣṇa-nāma*

**Kṛṣṇa-nāma is the sweetest of the sweet and the most auspicious of all that is auspicious. It is the greatest fruit in the tree of the** ***Vedas*****, and is naturally comprised of pure transcendence. O best of the Bhṛgus! If someone chants it once with faith or even with indifference he is immediately delivered. (*****Skanda Purāṇa*****)**

Thus, in the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛṭa*, the Lord states:

*kṛṣṇera yateka khelā, sarvottama nara-līlā,*  
*nara-vapu tāhāra svarūpa*  
*gopa-veśa, veṇu-kara, nava-kiśora, naṭa-vara,*  
*nara-līlāra haya anurūpa*

**The highest of all of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes are His human-like activities. His natural form is that of a human. In order to suitably perform His human-like play, He dresses like a cowherd boy holding a flute in His hand, has blossoming youth, and is an expert dancer. (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **21.101)**

It is essential for devotees to analyse this *līlā-tattva*; therefore, the Lord has spoken as follows:

*ataeva bhāgavata karaha vicāra*  
*ihā haite pābe sūtra-śrutira artha-sāra*

**Therefore, analyse the** ***Bhāgavatam*****, and by doing so, you will understand the essential meaning of the** ***Vedānta-sūtras*** **which are based on the** ***śruti*****. (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **25.153)**

In regards to *parokṣavāda*, the *Bhāgavata* contains this great instruction found in the words of Śrī Śuka to Parikṣita as follows:

*kathā imās te kathitā mahīyasāṁ*  
*vitāya lokeṣu yaśaḥ pareyuṣām*  
*vijñāna-vairāgya-vivakṣayā vibho*  
*vaco-vibhūtīr na tu pāramārthyam*

**O mighty one, I have told you these stories of great monarchs who attained fame in this world and departed, because I desired to cultivate realised knowledge and detachment in you. Such narrations are literal embellishments and have no bearing on the supreme goal of life. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **12.3.14)**

If pure *bhagavat-bhakti* does not arise from hearing and chanting all these narrations from the *Purāṇas*, then what is the benefit? Therefore, thoroughly analyse *līlā-tattva* and relish the sweetness of Kṛṣṇa. The *Gopāla Tāpanī Śruti* states:

*āvirbhāva-tirobhāvā sva-pade tiṣṭhati*  
*tāmasī rājasī sāttvikī mānuṣī*  
*vijñāna-ghanānanda-ghana-*  
*saccidānandaika-rase bhakti-yoge tiṣṭhati*

**This form, which appears and disappears, remains within His abode. This form, which appears amongst humans who are in the modes of goodness, passion and ignorance, is the embodiment of concentrated realised knowledge and bliss, and always remains in connection with** ***bhakti*****, which solely consists of** ***sac-cid-ānanda*****–*****rasa*****. (*****Uttara*** ***Gopāla Tāpanī Upaniṣad*** **95-97)**

This *śruti* has sufficiently explained the science of *avatāras*. The narrations of the *avatāras* are eternal and transcendental, yet they are neither historical or imaginary. Even if this is the fictitious accomplishment of poets, it must be regarded as material, because imagination relies upon material substances.

Both conscious and non-conscious substances have been created by the activity of some particular potency of the Supreme Lord. Although it is accepted that other energies manifest from the one divine potency, nevertheless, both consciousness and non-consciousness are so diametrically opposed to one another, that even devotee-analysts view consciousness as emanating from the *cit-śakti* and non-consciousness to be derived from the *māyā-śakti*. In regards to distinction between the Lord’s potencies, in actuality, there is no argument because one supreme potency, which is referred to as the power of the Lord, must be considered as a part of Him. Even though it is subordinate to the Lord, it cannot be imagined as a different substance or a different *tattva* from Him. At the time when the *cit-padartha* (conscious substance) is created, that potency manifests in its pure form, and at the time when *acit-padartha* (unconscious substance) arises, that potency is hindered by dense darkness. Thus, all those persons who quibble about the unity and plurality of potencies merely remain engaged in futile efforts. It must be accepted that at time of constructing a boat, a person adopts a certain frame of mind, and at the time of constructing a house, a different attitude arises. The capability for construction is the same potency – there are only different manifestations of thought. Thus, there is no conflict regarding the states of duality and non-duality in relation to the Infinite. Both conclusions are based on truth. However, many people, by perceiving a distinction between the potency of the Lord and the Lord Himself, turn away from pure analysis. Therefore, in the following *sūtra*, any conceptual difference between the potency of Bhagavān and the Supreme Reality has been eradicated.

**Комментарий:**

***para-śaktes tattvāntaratvaṁ pariharati***

That the potency is different from the Supreme Reality is now refuted.

## Sutra 7

*tac-chaktitas tattvādhikyam iti cen na tad-abhedāt*

**Перевод:**

**If it is said that the Supreme Reality is different to the potency, then it is not so because of non-difference.**

**Комментарий:**

***tasya parameśvarasya sṛṣṭi-kartṛtvādikaṁ śakty-apekṣaṁ cet śaktir api pṛthak tattvam astu ity āśaṅkāṁ pariharati tad-abhedāt iti. tasya parameśvarasya tābhiḥ śaktibhiḥ saha abhedāt śaktir na padārthāntaraṁ ‘śakti-śaktimator abheda’ iti nyāyāt. nānā-pramāṇāpekṣā nahy agner dāha-śaktir agni-bhinnatvenopalabhyate iti sarva-loka-siddhatvāt, tathāpi ‘svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca’ \[śve.u.6.8\] iti śrutir vartate.***

If one assumes that the Supreme Lord depends on His potency in order to create etc., then it might be suspected that the potency itself is a separate reality. This doubt is dispelled due to the principle of non-difference. Due to the non-difference of those potencies with the Supreme Lord, the potency is not a separate substance. Thus, Nyāya states, ‘The potency and the possessor of the potency are non-different.’ There is no evidence for any disctinction – it is universally recognised that the potency of fire to burn is not perceived as distinct from fire itself. Nevertheless, the *śruti* says, ‘He possesses intrinsic potencies of knowledge, strength and action’ (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.8)

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

There is no difference between the Lord and His opulence. Both of these are established as united within He who is the One Undivided Principle. Just as fire and its potency to burn are not independent, just as a diamond and hardness are inseparable, just as the body and its limbs are one integrated substance, just as the sun and its radiance are not two different substances, similarly, there is no possibility of duality between the Supreme Lord and His supreme potency. With all these examples, worldly comparisons do not reveal the pure truth, since no similarity can be observed between the Lord and the universe. When a devotee perceives the Lord, this non-duality is completely revealed in his mind. Thus, we find in *Śrī Viṣṇu Purāṇa*:

*eka-deśa-sthitasyāgner jyotsnā vistāriṇī yathā*  
*parasya brahmaṇaḥ śaktis tathedam akhilaṁ jagat*

**Just as the effulgence of fire, though situated in one place, spreads everywhere, similarly, the power of the Supreme pervades the entire world. (*****Viṣṇu Purāṇa*** **1.22.54)**

Indeed, in the *Devi Mahātmyam* of the *Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa*, the *ṛṣi* speaks thus:

*etat te kathitaṁ bhūpa devī-māhātmyam uttamam*  
*evaṁ prabhāvā sā devī yayedaṁ dhāryate jagat*  
*vidyā tathaiva kriyate bhagavad-viṣṇu-māyayā*  
*tayā tvam eṣa vaiśyaś ca tathaivānye vivekinaḥ*

**O king, now I shall describe the** ***Devi Mahātmyam*** **unto you. By her influence that goddess maintains the world. She is the potency of Lord Viṣṇu. Also, knowledge is imparted by her who is the** ***māya*** **potency of Bhagavān Viṣṇu. From her, many persons who possess discrimination, including this merchant, have attained knowledge. (*****Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa*** **93.1)**

Also, in the Second Rātra, Third Chapter of the *Nārada Pañcarātra*, Mahādeva states:

*eka īśaḥ prathamato dvidha-rūpo babhūva saḥ*  
*eka strī viṣṇu-māyā yā pumān ekaḥ svayaṁ vibhuḥ*

**Initially, the one Supreme Being appeared in two forms – one is female, the** ***māyā*** **of Viṣṇu, and the other as male, that one infinite Personality Himself. (*****Nārada Pañcarātra*** **2.3.24)**

Through all these *ślokas*, the non-duality of the potency and the Possessor of the potency is established. The potency, being subservient, is conceived of in a female form, and has become the recipient of the embrace of the conscious Possessor of potency. In order to make the Supreme Reality clear and accessible to the mind, to a certain extent, the *brahmaṛṣis* have provided ornamental descriptions in relation to this concept within their prayers. Actually, Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa are the sole Supreme Truth.

**Комментарий:**

***nanu parameśvarasya viśva-sṛṣṭy-ādi-kartṛtve vikāritvaṁ prasajjetety āśaṅkāṁ nirasyati***

“However, the activities of creation etc., may imply that the Supreme Lord undergoes some transformation” This doubt is disproven thus –

## Sutra 8

*kartāpy-avikāraḥ svātantryāt*

**Перевод:**

**The Active Agent remains unchanged due to His independent nature**

**Комментарий:**

***loke yaḥ kartā bhavati, sa rāga-dveṣādi-vikāravān bhavati. iti sva-kṛta-niyame svasya svatantratvāt tādṛśa-niyamādhīnatvābhāvāt sa parameśvaro jagat-kartāpi vikāra-rahitaḥ. ‘niṣkalaṁ niṣkriyaṁ śāntaṁ niravadyaṁ nirañjanaṁ’ iti śruteḥ.***

One who becomes a doer within this world is subject to transformations such as attachment, aversion etc. The Supreme Lord is independent and not subject to the injunctions that He Himself has created. Even as the creator of the world, He remains devoid of change. Thus, the *śruti* says, ‘He is beyond time, beyond material activities, is tranquil, without defects and without contamination.’ (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.19)

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

Whatever rules are observed in the world are all created by the Lord. Due to the *acintya-śakti* (inconceivable potency) of the Supreme Lord, all these rules are inextricable. The inviolability of all these rules must also be considered as the glories of the Lord. There are many kinds of rules. Among them, physical, mental, spiritual and worldly rules etc. are always observed in this material world. These rules are all-powerful always. For example, when wood comes in contact with fire, the wood burns – this is a physical law. If a decision is made on any topic without conducting a proper analysis, it goes against mental laws because it only creates confusion. Stealing the property of others, immorality, and lies are all contrary to spiritual laws. Anyone who engages in any activity, whatever it may be, must inevitably reap the consequences. Human beings do not possesses the capacity to act against these particular laws. It is a physical law that by connecting one cubit of rope to another cubit of rope, it will result in being two cubits in length – it will never become three cubits. However, the Supreme Lord is not bound by all these laws. He is the creator of all rules, thus He is not subject to His own rules. The *Kaṭhopaniṣad* says:

*anyatra dharmād anyatrādharmād anyatrāsmāt kṛtākṛtāt*  
*anyatra bhūtāca bhavyāc ca yat tat paśyasi tadvada*

**The Absolute Truth is beyond** ***dharma*** **and** ***adharma*****, He is distinct from this world of cause and effect and He is different from past, present and future. Please explain to me how you perceive Him. (*****Kaṭhopaniṣad*** **1.2.14)**

In the Ninth Chapter of the Tenth Canto of the *Śrīmad Bhāgavata* it is stated:

*na cāntar na bahir yasya na pūrvaṁ nāpi cāparam*  
*pūrvāparaṁ bahiś cāntar jagato yo jagac ca yaḥ*  
*taṁ matvātmajam avyaktaṁ martya-liṅgam adhokṣajam*  
*gopikolūkhale dāmnā babandha prākṛtaṁ yathā*  
*tad dāma badhyamānasya svārbhakasya kṛtāgasaḥ*  
*dvy-aṅgulonam abhūt tena sandadhe ‘nyac ca gopikā*  
*yadāsīt tad api nyūnaṁ tenānyad api sandadhe*  
*tad api dvy-aṅgulaṁ nyūnaṁ yad yad ādatta bandhanam*

**He has no interior, no exterior, no before or after. He pervades the world in all directions, inside and outside, and He is the world itself. Yet He who is beyond the material senses and is unmanifest has appeared like an ordinary human child and has been bound to a grinding-mortar by that** ***gopikā*** **(Yaśodā) who considers Him to be her son. When she attempted to bind her naughty child, she found that the rope for binding Him became short by two fingers, so she joined another rope to it. When even that rope was joined, it was still short by two fingers, and when she joined yet another rope to it, it was still two fingers too short. Thus, any ropes she tied together to bind Him, remained short by two fingers. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **10.9.13-16)**

The independence of the Supreme Lord is being revealed through this sacred description. Whichever person becomes an active agent certainly becomes subject to transformation in regards to desire. This too is a law of the Supreme Lord. However, since the Supreme Lord Himself is not bound by the such a law, He remains unchangable even while creating the relationship between the conscious and the non-conscious.

**Комментарий:**

***viśva-sṛṣṭi-pralayābhyāṁ tasya vṛddhi-hrāsābhāvau sūcayati***

That He does not increase or decrease when creating and annihilating the universe will now be pointed out.

## Sutra 9

*sadaika-rūpaḥ pūrṇatvāt*

**Перевод:**

**(Due to His being fully complete, His form always remains the same)**

**Комментарий:**

***anirvacanīya-brahmāṇḍa-racanāyāṁ viśva-pralaye’pi sadā parameśvarasya eka-rūpatvaṁ vṛddhi-hrāsau na bhavata ity arthaḥ. yathā nadyādi-vṛddhi-hrāsābhyāṁ samudrasyopacayāpacayo na staḥ. tatra hetuḥ—tasya parameśvarasya pūrṇatvād iti. ‘pūrṇam adaḥ pūrṇam idaṁ pūrṇāt pūrṇam udacyate’ iti śruteḥ.***

Even during the indescribable processes of universal creation and dissolution, the Supreme Lord’s uniformity always remains unchanged, meaning that there is no increase or decrease in Him. This is like an ocean which does not experience increase or decrease due to the influx or outflux of rivers. The reason is due to the Supreme Lord completeness. As the *śruti* says, ‘ The Supreme is complete and His creation is complete. Whatever manifests from Him is complete.’ (*Īśopaniṣad*)

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

The inherent nature of that Supreme Lord is complete at all times. There is no increase of decrease in Him during universal creation, maintenance or annihilation. The Supreme Lord is full in all opulence. Thus, the prayers of the *Vedas* say thus:

*jaya jaya jahy ajām ajita doṣa-gṛbhīta-guṇāṁ*  
*tvam asi yad ātmanā samavaruddha-samasta-bhagaḥ*  
*aga-jagad-okasām akhila-śakty-avabodhaka te*  
*kvacid ajayātmanā ca carato ‘nucaren nigamaḥ*

**You destroy the** ***māyā-śakti*** **known as** ***ajā*** **(eternal) that controls the modes of nature that are the basis of all difficulties. Through Your** ***ātmā-śakti*** **(personal potency) You are eternally the Lord of all potencies. You control the energy of everything that is animate and inanimate. The entire Vedas describe You in two ways, namely when You engage with the** ***māyā-śakti*** **it is described in one way, and when You engage with Your** ***ātmā-śakti*** **or** ***cit-śakti*** **for performing** ***vraja-līlā*****, then You are described in another way. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **10.87.14)**

The Supreme Lord is always complete, yet a doubt arises regarding the topic of Him being the creator of the universe – what kind of inclination does He have in creating consciousness and non-consciousness, and what is the reason for such an action? It has been clarified thus.

**Комментарий:**

***pūrṇa-rūpasya viśva-sṛṣṭy-ādi-kartṛtve ko hetur ity apekṣāyām āha***

In regards to the cause of the Supreme Lord, who is complete, engaging as the agent for creating the universe etc., it is explained thus –

## Sutra 10

*kāruṇyaṁ tat-kriyā-hetur nānyad āpta-kāmatvāt*

**Перевод:**

**The cause of such an activity is compassion. There is no other reason since He has no desires to fulfil.**

**Комментарий:**

***tasya parameśvarasya sṛṣṭy-ādi-kriyāyāṁ pravṛtti-hetu-kāruṇyaṁ karuṇā-vilāsa eva anyat kāraṇāntaraṁ nāsti āpta-kāmatvāt. jīvānāṁ hi tat tat kāmas tayā tat tat karmaṇi pravṛttir bhavati, ‘ātmanaḥ kāmāya sarvaṁ priyaṁ bhavati’ iti śruteḥ, īśvarasya na tathā āpta-kāmatvāt pūrṇa-kāmatvād ity arthaḥ. ‘satya-kāmaḥ satya-saṅkalpaḥ’ iti śruteḥ. ‘nānavāptam avāptavyam’ iti smṛteś ca.***

For the Supreme Lord, the motivational cause of activities such as creation etc. is only a pastime of compassion; there is no other cause, since He has no desires to fulfil. Indeed, for the *jīvas*, due to their particular desires, they engage in corresponding activities. As stated in the *śruti*, ‘For the sake of one’s own desires, everything becomes dear.’ (*Bṛhad-Ārāṇyaka Upaniṣad* 2.4.5, 4.5.6). It is not so for the Lord because He has no desires to fulfil, in other words, He is complete in His desires. The *śruti* says, ‘His desires are infallible. His will is infallible.’ (*Chāndogya Upaniṣad* 8.7.1) And the *smṛti* states, ‘I lack nothing nor do I need to gain anything.’ (*Bhagavad-gītā* 3.22).

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

Everyone harbours doubts in regards to the *līlā* of that Personality whose desires are ever-fulfilled. Nevertheless, in Seventh Chapter of the Third Canto of the *Bhāgavata*, there is a question posed by Vidura:

*brahman kathaṁ bhagavatas cin-mātrasyāvikāriṇaḥ*  
*līlayā cāpi yujyeran nirguṇasya guṇāḥ kriyāḥ*  
*krīḍāyām udyamo’rbhasya kāmaś cikrīḍiṣānyataḥ*  
*svatas-tṛptasya ca kathaṁ nivṛttasya sadānyataḥ*

**O** ***brāhmaṇa*****, how is it that Bhagavān is unchangeable, fully spiritual and** ***nirguṇa*** **interact with the** ***guṇas*****, even if it is simply His pastime? Desire is the motivation for children to play with other children. Boys are enthusiastic to play with other boys or with various diversions because they are encouraged by desire. How can desire arise in He who is always detached and self-satisifed? (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **3.7.2-3)**

*śrī maitreya uvāca*  
*seyaṁ bhagavato māyā yan nayena virudhyate*

**Śrī Maitreya replied: The** ***māyā*** **potency of Bhagavān is contrary to logic. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **3.7.9)**

In the commentary to this, it is said:

*bhagavato’cintya śakterīśasya seyaṁ māyā nayena tarkena viruddhyata iti*

**The** ***māyā*** **of Bhagavān, who possesses inconceivable potencies is opposed to reason or logic. (Śrīdhara Svāmī)**

Just as this question is profound, the answer is equally satisfying. Maitreya said, “O Vidura! You have asked a difficult question, the answer to which cannot be provided by any *jīva*. Thus, it is necessary to have faith in the pastimes of Bhagavān. Nothing can be conclusively decided about that topic through logic. Logic cannot determine anything in regards to that Infinite Principle or His activities. It can only be accepted.”

Thus, it is said in the *Bhāgavata*:

*sa vā idaṁ viśvam amogha-līlaḥ*  
*sṛjaty avaty atti na sajjate ‘smin*  
*bhūteṣu cāntarhita ātma-tantraḥ*  
*ṣāḍ-vargikaṁ jighrati ṣaḍ-guṇeśaḥ*

**He whose pastimes are pure, creates, maintains and dissolves this universe. He Himself is fully independent and enters into all beings. As the Lord of the six senses, he contacts the six sense-objects and remains unaffected by them. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **1.3.36)**

This universe itself intrinsically supports His *līlā*; therefore, it can be described as a product of His pastimes. However, what is the Lord’s self-interest in regards to performing His pastimes? The answer to this question is that He has no self-interest; the cause of these pastimes is merely compassion towards conscious entities. Thus, the *śruti* says:

*ānando brahmeti vyajānāt. ānandoddhy eva khilvimāni bhūtāni jāyante*  
*ānandena jātāni jīvanti ānandaṁ prayanty abhisaṁviśantī*

**One should know that the Supreme is bliss itself. All beings are produced from that bliss alone. Being born, they live by that bliss. They eventually enter that bliss and again return. (*****Taittirīya Upaniṣad*** **3.6)**

## Chapter 2. Cit Padārtha Prakaraṇam (The Chapter Concerning That Which is Conscious)

## Sutra 11

*cetanāḥ parānugatās tad-vidhi-vaśyatvāt*

**Перевод:**

**Beings endowed with consciousness follow His injunctions because they are dependent upon Him**

**Комментарий:**

***atha cetnāś caitanya-viśiṣṭā jīvā bahu-vacanopadeśāt te ca bahavaḥ, kintu parasya īśvarasya anugatās tena niyamitās tad-adhīnā ity arthaḥ, tat-kṛta-vidhi-vaśyatvāt. ‘ya ātmani tiṣṭhan ātmānam antar yamayati’ iti śruteḥ \| ‘īśvaraḥ sarva-bhūtānāṁ hṛd-deśe’rjuna tiṣṭhati’ iti smṛteḥ.***

Here, *cetana* refers to the *jīvas* because they are endowed with consciousness. According to rules of grammar, the plural form (*cetanāḥ*) has been used because they (the *jīvas*) are many. However, they are followers of and subordinate to the Supreme Lord, and are controlled by Him. This is the meaning. They are under the control of His regulations. As stated in the *śruti*, ‘That Supreme Ātmā resides within to regulate the *ātmā’* (*Bṛhad-Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad* 3.7.22) Similarly, as the *smṛti* states, ‘The Supreme Lord resides in the hearts of all beings, O Arjuna.’ (*Bhagavad-gītā* 18.61)

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

Some Vedāntists hold the view that the *jīvātma* is a single substance, but manifests in various forms through different mediums. To refute this illogical conclusion, the plural form of the word ‘consciousness’ (*cetana*) has been used to refer to the individual souls. All these *jīvas* are subservient to the Lord since they are all governed by His rules. In this regard, it is mentioned in the *Nārada Pañcarātra*, in the first chapter of the Second Ratri, through the words of Sadā-Śiva:

*jīvas tat-pratibimbaś ca bhoktā ca sukha-duḥkhayoḥ*  
*kecid vadanti taṁ nityaṁ kāraṇasya guṇena ca*  
*vidyamānāt tirodhānaṁ tirodhānāc ca sambhavaḥ*  
*dehād dehāntaraṁ yāti na mṛtyus tasya kutracit*

**Some say that the** ***jīvas*** **are reflections of Him and the experiencers of happiness and distress, and that they are eternal due to the qualities of the cause (the Supreme Lord). From existence comes disappearance, and from disappearance comes rebirth. The** ***jīva*** **travels from one body to another; death never befalls him anywhere. (*****Nārada Pañcarātra*** **2.1.28-29)**

It is also said in the seventh chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā*:

*apareyam itas tvanyāṁ prakṛtiṁ viddhi me parām*  
*jīva-bhūtāṁ mahā-bāho yayedaṁ dhāryate jagat*  
*etad yonīni bhūtāni sarvāṇīty-upadhāraya*  
*ahaṁ kṛtsnasya jagataḥ prabhavaḥ pralayas tathā*

**However, you should know that there is another nature, superior to this inferior nature. It is a conscious potency that consists of the living beings and maintains the universe. Try to understand that all beings are manifest from these two sources and that I am the cause of the creation and annihilation of the entire universe. (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **7.5-6)**

It is also said in the *Upaniṣad*:

*śvetaketo tat-tvam-asi*

**O Śvetaketu, you are that. (*****Chāndogya Upaniṣad*** **6.8.7)**

The guru tells the disciple, “O Śvetaketu, *tat-tvam-asi*.’ Some Vedāntists say that this means, “O Śvetaketu, you are that Brahman whom you seek.” However, Gauḍācārya Pūrṇānanda Svāmī has written in the book, *Tattva Muktāvalī Māyāvāda Śata Duṣaṇī* thus:

*sākṣāt tat tvam asīti veda-viṣaye vākyaṁ tu yad vidyate*  
*tasyārthaṁ kurute svakīya-mata-vid bhede’rpayitvā matim*  
*tac-chabdo’vyayam eva bhedaka iti taṁ tatra bhedyo yataḥ*  
*ṣaṣṭhī-lopa-mitā tam eva na hi tad-vākyārtha etādṛśaḥ*

**The statement,** ***tat-tvam-asi*** **is directly found in the** ***Vedas*****, but, those who are proponents of their own interpretations have given it a different meaning. However, the word** ***tat*** **refers to the Infallible Lord, which distinguishes Him from** ***tvam*** **(you). Distinction must be there because of the use of the** ***ṣaṣṭhī-tat-puruṣa-samāsa*** **(the sixth possessive compound in Sanskrit grammar), so** ***tat*** **cannot refer to** ***tvam*****, but means ‘you belong to Him.’ The meaning of the words is like this. (*****Tattva Muktāvalī*** **6)**

In reality, the guru is telling the disciple, “O Śvetaketu, you belong to that Supreme Lord,” meaning that you have been created and controlled by that Supreme Lord. Alternatively, even if one does not refute the interpretation of the Vivartavādīs**,** who claim that “You are that Brahman,” then it means that Brahman cannot be realised by *acit-padārtha* (an unconscious substance). You yourself must be *cit-padārtha* (a conscious substance), thus you must seek Him out by being established in your own *svarūpa*.

Moreover, the view held by the devotees is also expressed in the *Caitanya-candrodaya-nāṭaka*

***vāsudeva-parā devatā vāsudeva-parāt parātmanaḥ saṅkarṣaṇo jīvaḥ ity ādi. jīvayati jīvaṁ karotīti jīvaḥ. na tu svayaṁ jīvaḥ. sa cātmā śabda-brahma para-brahma mamobhe śāśvatī tanū iti tad-ukteḥ. tasmād eva jīva-sṛṣṭir ity arthaḥ.***

**Vāsudeva is the Supreme Divinity. From Vāsudeva come the Paramātmā, and Saṅkarṣaṇa from whom the** ***jīvas*** **manifest. “He who gives life to the** ***jīvas*** **is also a** ***jīva*****” – No! He Himself is not a** ***jīva*****. That Supreme Ātmā has stated, “Śabda-brahma and Parabrahma are both My eternal forms.” Thus, He has created the** ***jīva*** **– that is the meaning. (*****Caitanya-candrodaya-nāṭaka*** **6.258)**

The next *sūtra* will determine the eternality or non-eternality of the *jīva*.

**Комментарий:**

*nanu ‘ayamātmā brahmetyādi’ śrutiṣu jīvānāṁ brahmābhinnatvā pratipādanena katham atra jīvānām īśvarādhīnatvaṁ sūtrakāreṇa niścitam ity āha*

**But, in the statements of śruti such as, ‘The ātmā is Brahman’ (Bṛhad-Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.5) the non-difference between the jīvas and Brahman is affirmed. How, then, does the author of the sūtras determine the dependence of the jīvas on the Lord?**

## Sutra 12

*te cānādy-anantaḥ para-śakti-viśeṣatvāt*

**Перевод:**

**They are beginningless and eternal due to their nature of being special manifestations of the Lord’s potency**

**Комментарий:**

***te ca jīvā anādayo’nantāś ca yataḥ parameśvarasya śakti-rūpās tac-chakter ādy-anta-rahitatvāt ‘yathāgner bahavo visphuliṅgā’ iti śruteḥ. ‘mamaivāṁśo jīva-loke jīva-bhūta’ iti smṛteś ca.***

And those *jīvas* are beginningless and eternal because they are forms of the Supreme Lord’s potency. That potency is also without beginning or end, as illustrated by the *śruti* which states, ‘They are like many sparks from a fire’ (*Bṛhad-Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad* 2.1.20) and also the *smṛti*, which says, “The living beings of this world are My eternal particles.” (*Bhagavad-gīta* 15.7)

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

There is much controversy concerning the *jīva’s* existence. Some claim that the *jīva* is eternal, as stated by Śiva in the *Nārada Pañcarātra:*

*kecid vadanti taṁ nityaṁ kāraṇasya guṇena ca*

**Some say that it is eternal due to the qualities of its cause. (*****Nārada Pañcarātra*** **2.1.28)**

Again, Śiva says:

*kecid vadanty anityaṁ ca mithyaiva kṛtrimaḥ sadā*  
*pralīyate punas tatra pratibimbo yathā raveḥ*

**Some say that it is temporary, false, and artificial, always dissolving back into that from which it arose, like the reflection of the sun.** ***(Nārada Pañcarātra*** **2.1.31)**

The debate over whether the *jīva* is eternal or non-eternal is unnecessary because the *jīva* can be called both *nitya* (eternal) and *anitya* (non-eternal). The very cause of the *jīva* is the potency of the Supreme Lord, and that potency is eternal, without beginning and without end. Therefore, based on the qualities of the cause, the eternality of the *jīva* can be accepted. The power by which Jagadīśvara, the Lord of the universe, has created the *jīvas* is called the *jiva-śakti*, in other words it is *Saṅkarṣaṇa*.

In the *Gītā*, Bhagavān says:

*apareyam itas tv anyaṁ prakṛtiṁ viddhi me parām*  
*jīva-bhūtāṁ mahā-bāho yayedaṁ dhāryate jagat*

**However, you should know that there is another nature, superior to this inferior nature. It is a conscious potency that consists of the living beings and maintains the universe. (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **7.5)**

The *jīva*, which is the result of this beginningless and endless potency, is eternal by the quality of its cause. However, the will of the Supreme Lord is the most powerful of all, and if ever the Lord desires to dissolve the *jīva*, then its dissolution will certainly take place. That is why the *jīva* is also called *anitya* (not eternal). When the *jīva* is accepted as resulting from the *jiva-śakti*, then the beginninglessness and endlessness of its Cause can also be attributed to it.

The *Gītā* says:

*avyaktādīni bhūtāni vyakta-madhyāni bhārata*  
*avyakta-nidhanāny eva tatra kā parivedanā*

**O Bhārata, all living beings are unmanifest before birth, manifest between birth and death, and again unmanifest after death. What then is the reason for lamentation? (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.28)**

The eighteenth *mantra* of the second part of the first chapter of *Kaṭhopaniṣad* states:

*na jāyate mriyate vā vipaścin*  
*nāyaṁ kutaścin na babhūva kaścit*  
*ajo nityaḥ śāśvato ‘yaṁ purāṇo*  
*na hanyate hanyamāne śarīre*

**One who is wise never takes birth nor dies at any time. Similarly, the Supreme is not born nor does He die. The living being is unborn, eternal, indestructible and timeless – he is not destroyed when the material body is destroyed.**

By the attribute found in this *sūtra*, the *jīva’s* *brahma-svarūpatva* (identity as *Brahman, or spirit)* is established. Even though the *jīva* is endowed with  the nature of *Brahman*, that Parabrahman or Parameśvara, possesses different characteristics from the *jīva*. This is illustrated in the next *sūtra*.

**Комментарий:**

***jīvānāṁ para-śakti-viśeṣa-rūpatve’bheda evāpadyata ity āśaṅkāyāṁ bhedaṁ dṛḍhīkaroti***

“Since the *jīvas* are manifestations of the supreme potency, there is no difference them.” This doubt concerning distinction (between the *jīva* and the Supreme) will be firmly dealt with.

## Sutra 13

*cid-ānanda-svarūpā api parato bhinnā nitya-satyatvābhāvāt*

**Перевод:**

**Although the jīvas inherently possess the nature of consciousness and bliss, they are different from the Supreme due to the absence of eternal existence**

**Комментарий:**

***te jīvāś cid-ānanda-svarūpā api parataḥ parameśvarāt bhinnāḥ. tatra hetuḥ – nitya-satyatvābhāvād iti. tatreyaṁ prakriyā jīvānāṁ satyatve’pi teṣāṁ sattā-pradaḥ parameśvara eva nitya-satyaḥ, na tu te tathā. ‘nityo nityānām’ iti, ‘satyasya satyam’ iti ‘parāt param’ ityādi-śruteḥ. ‘nirdvandvo nitya-sattva-sthaḥ’ iti smṛteś ca.***

Although the *jīvas* have the nature of consciousness and bliss, they are different from the Supreme Lord. The reason for this difference is the absence of eternal existence. In this context, even though the *jīvas* possess some form of existence, it is the Supreme Lord alone who is eternally existent who grants existence to them, and not the *jīvas* themselves. Therefore, the *śruti* states, ‘He is the Eternal amongst eternals’ (*Kaṭhopaniṣad* 2.2.13), ‘He is the Reality amongst realities’ (*Bṛhad-Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad* 2.1.20), ‘He is the Supreme amongst those that are supreme.’ (*Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad* 3.2.8). The *smṛti* also says, ‘Beyond duality and situated in eternal existence’ (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.45).

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

The intrinsic nature of the *jīva* is *cid-ānanda* (consciousness and bliss), while the intrinsic nature of Brahman is *sac-cid-ānanda* (existence, consciousness, and bliss). The statement in the *Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad*, *dvā suparṇā sayujā sakhāyā* (‘two bird-companions sit together’), establishes that the *jīva* and Brahman, while residing together, share the same nature. The true meaning of ‘sharing the same nature’ is that both are of the intrinsic nature of *cid-ānanda*. After discussing all these topics, persons with an immature understanding see no difference between Brahman and the *jīva*. Although the *jīva* is indeed of the nature of Brahman, he does not attain the full nature of Brahman, since Brahman itself is unchanging and imperishable. However, the *jīva*, having emanated from the Supreme Lord’s *jīva-śakti*, does transform. For this reason, in some specific aspect, there is a particular distinction between the *jīva* and Brahman.

Thus, it is stated in second *mantra* of the third section of the *Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad*:

*samāne vṛkṣe puruṣo nimagno’nīśayā śocati muhyamānaḥ*  
*juṣṭaṁ yadā paśyaty anyam īśam asya mahimānam iti vīta-śokaḥ*

**On the same tree, the** ***puruṣa*** **(the enjoying** ***jīva*****) who is immersed in his own desires grieves and is deluded. But when he sees the glories of the other, the Lord, then he becomes free from sorrow.**

As long as the *jīva* continues to experience the results of its own actions, he remains in distress because he is inherently weak, powerless, and incomplete. However, when the *jīva* takes shelter in the Supreme Lord, he no longer suffers. This *śruti* establishes that the *jīva* lacks completeness, whereas the Supreme possesses it. The *jīva* possesses existence, but not ultimate eternal existence. The *jīva’s* existence is dependent upon the will of the Supreme Lord. Therefore, although the *jīva* exists, it is not eternal existence, and although he is eternal, he is not eternally eternal. This is the distinction between the *jīva* and the Lord. The *jīva* is finite consciousness, but the Supreme Lord is eternal consciousness. Although the previous *sūtra* acknowledges the beginninglessness and eternality of the *jīva*, there is an inherent difference between the *jīva* and the Supreme Lord. Some followers of *Vedānta* accept a distinction between the *jīva* and the Supreme Lord due to the impositions on the *jīva*, yet they still establish Advaitavāda (non-dualism). Therefore, the next two *sūtras* are intended to explain and resolve the various opinions of such interpreters.

**Комментарий:**

***bhedābheda-vicāra-hetukaṁ sampradāya-bhedaṁ nirūpayati***

(The following *sūtras*) address the cause of considering difference and non- difference among other *sampradāyas*.

## Sutra 14

*teṣāṁ paratvaṁ kecid apare bhedam itare tūbhayam*

**Перевод:**

**Some state that the jīva is the Supreme, others say there is difference, and some accept both**

**Комментарий:**

***teṣāṁ jīvānāṁ paratvaṁ brahma-rūpatvaṁ kecid bādarāyaṇādyāḥ pratipādayanti. apare kaśyapādayas tu bhedaṁ teṣāṁ parameśvara-bhinnatvaṁ vadanti. itare śāṇḍilyādayaḥ kenacid aṁśena bhedaṁ kenacid aṁśena abhedaṁ ca vyācakṣate. tatra yathāyathaṁ pramāṇāny api darśitāni. ‘ayamātmā brahmeti.’ ‘dvā suparṇā sayuje sakhāyā’ iti, ‘ekadhā bahudhā caiva dṛśyate jala-candravat’ ityādi-śrutayaḥ***

Some, such as Bādārayana, establish the spiritual status of the *jīvas* as Brahman. Others, such as Kaśyapa, assert difference, stating that the *jīvas* are distinct from the Supreme Lord. Others like Śāṇḍilya, explain that some view a partial difference and others view a partial non-difference.  Thus, evidence has been presented as appropriate. The *śruti* states, ‘The *ātmā* is Brahman’ (*Bṛhad-Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad* 4.4.5), ‘Two bird-companions sit together’ (*Munḍaka Upaniṣad* 3.1.1), ‘He is perceived as one and as many, just like the moon reflected in water’ (*Amṛta Bindu Upaniṣad* 12).

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

In relation to the *jīva*, there are three kinds of opinions that are seen amongst the Āryans, namely Dvaita (dualism), Advaita (non-dualism) and Dvaitādvaita (the combination of dualism and non-dualism). Dvaitavādīs such as Kaśyapa argue that just as the Lord is an eternal substance, the *jīva* is also eternal, and both the *jīva* and Brahman are eternally distinct. The support for their view is found in the third section of the *Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad*, which states:

*dvā suparṇā sayujā sakhāyā samānaṁ vṛkṣaṁ praiṣasvajāte*  
*tayor anyaḥ pippalaṁ svādv atty anaśnann anyo’bhicakāśīti*

**Two bird-companions sit together in the same tree. One of them eats the sweet fruit of the tree, while the other looks on without eating. (*****Munḍaka Upaniṣad*** **3.1.1)**

Some consider the *jīva* to be a transformation of Brahman itself and do not recognise any real difference between the *jīva* and Brahman. The following *mantra* of the *Kaṭhopaniṣad* supports their opinion:

*asya visraṁśamānasya śarīra-sthasya dehinaḥ*  
*dehād vimucyamānasya kim atra pariśiṣyate*  
*etad vai tat*

**When the embodied** ***jīva*** **gives up the material body, and is freed from the bodily condition, what is it that remains? (Yama replied:) It is this. (*****Kaṭhopaniṣad*** **2.2.4)**

Sages like Śāṇḍilya accept that, at present, the *jīva* and Brahman are indeed distinct, but that it is possible for the *jīva* to attain Brahman through the process of liberation. Therefore, that substance which is presently dualistic achieves non-dualism. Regarding this, the *śruti* says:

*sarvaṁ khalv idaṁ brahma taj-jalān iti śānta upāsīta*

**Indeed, everything is Brahman. Everything is created, maintained and dissolved by Him. A peaceful person should worship Him. (*****Chāndogya Upaniṣad*** **3.14)**

The *Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad* says:

*prāṇo hy eṣa yaḥ sarva-bhūtair vibhāti vijānan vidvān bhavate nātivādī*  
*ātma-krīḍa ātma-ratiḥ kriyāvān eṣa brahma-vidāṁ variṣṭhaḥ*

**That which pervades all living beings as their vital force, is known by the wise who understand to be the Ultimate Reality. Such a wise person is not given to excessive debate, is immersed in the joy of the self, and is devoted to the self. He is the foremost among those who have knowledge of Brahman. (*****Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad*** **3.1.4)**

**Комментарий:**

***nanv evaṁ mata-bheda-darśanena prāṇināṁ buddhi-bhrama eva syād ity āśaṅkāyāṁ sarveṣām aikamatya-rūpaṁ sva-mataṁ prakāśayati***

However, in response to the concern that so many different philosophical opinions regarding the living entities creates bewilderment, one conclusion which unites all individual viewpoints will now be revealed.

## Sutra 15

*sarveṣāṁ sāmañjasyaṁ sātvata-vijñānasya bhramatvābhāvāt pramāṇa-sad-bhāvāc ca*

**Перевод:**

**There is harmony amongst all opinions, due to divine knowledge being valid evidence which is free from error**

**Комментарий:**

***sarveṣām ṛṣīṇāṁ sāmañjasyam aika-matyam eva vicāreṇādhigamyate, teṣāṁ sātvatānāṁ bhagavat-tattva-jñānīnāṁ jñānasya bhramatvābhāvāt ayathārthābhāvāt, tan-mateṣu pūrvokta-śruty-ādi-pramāṇa-sad-bhāvād-apītyarthaḥ. ‘māyāṁ madīyām udgṛhya vadatāṁ kiṁ nu durghaṭaṁ’ iti śrī-bhagavad-ukteḥ***

Through careful analysis, harmony and unity among all the *ṛṣis* can be achieved. Amongst those who are noble, it is understood that knowledge concerning *bhagavat-tattva* is free from error and misinterpretation. This understanding is even supported by previously quoted evidence from the *śruti* and other authentic sources. But as *Śrī Bhāgavata* states, ‘Is it so difficult for those quibblers who have taken refuge in My *māyā* potency to argue?’(*Śrīmad Bhāgavatam* 11.22.4)

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

Evidence from the *śruti* has been shown for all three aforementioned opinions, therefore all must be considered true. In particular, Kaśyapa, Bādarāyaṇa, and Śāṇḍilya are all devotees of Bhagavān, that is, they are capable of realising the presence of Divinity (*bhagavad-bhāva*) through direct experience. Therefore, conclusions based on their self-evident convictions can never be erroneous. The differences that appear in their views on this topic are not real. They are all in agreement; only those who have established *sampadāyas* based on their teachings quarrel over a few statements. The Supreme Lord is the sole *advaya-tattva*, that Reality who is one without a second, and His potency is infinite. Among these potencies, the *jīva-śakti* and the *māyā-śakti* are familiar to the *jīvas*. By a transformation of that *jīva-śakti*, all the *jīvas* have been created and are existing at present. Later, according to the will of the Supreme Lord, they may also cease to exist. This is only established by two evidences – *pratyakṣa* (direct perception) and *anumāna* (inference). The *Taittirīya Upaniṣad* states:

*yato vā imāni bhūtāni jāyante yena jatāni jīvanti yat prayanty abhisamviśanti*

**From Him all beings are born, they live according to Him, and again they enter into Him. (*****Taittirīya Upaniṣad*** **3.1.1)**

Through this conclusion, the standpoint of Advaita is established since no other reality apart from Brahman is perceived. The concept of Dvaita is also established since the *jīva* and inert matter are not presently perceived to be false like a dream. This can also be said to support the view of Dvaitadvaita since both Advaita is seen at the beginning and the end, and Dvaita is perceived in the intermediate stages. Actually, there is no disagreement among the *ṛṣis* who wrote their *sūtras*. Conflicts only arise amongst those who have authored fictitious  commentaries and their argumentative disciples.

**Комментарий:**

***evaṁ jīva-svarūpaṁ nirūpya idānīṁ sarvānartha-nivṛtti-pūrvakaṁ paramārtha-phala-prāptaye upāyaṁ vaktum upakramate.***

Having now established the *jīva’s svarūpa*, we now proceed to discuss the means for attaining the ultimate goal, which involves the removal of all *anarthas* (unnecessary impediments) beforehand.

## Sutra 16

*vicāra-rāgau cetana-dharmau svarūpa-pravṛtti-bhāvāt*

**Перевод:**

**Analysis and attraction are the nature of the conscious jīva, because they are the tendencies of his svarūpa**

**Комментарий:**

***vicāro hi jñāna-janyaḥ, ata eva cetana-niṣṭhaḥ jñānasya tat svarūpatvāt rāgasyāpy ānanda-janyatvāt, ānandasya nija-rūpatvāt, cetana-niṣṭhatvaṁ tat-pravṛtti-rūpatvāc ca. ‘satyaṁ jñānam ānandam’ iti śruteḥ***

Analysis arises from knowledge; therefore, it is rooted in consciousness, since knowledge is the very nature of consciousness. Similarly, attachment (*rāga*) arises from bliss (*ānanda*), as bliss is inherent to one’s true nature. Hence, attachment is also rooted in consciousness, and it manifests as its dynamic expression. The *śruti* states, ‘The Supreme is eternality, knowledge and bliss’ (*Taittirīya Upaniṣad* 2.1.2)

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

Analysis and attachment are the inherent qualities of a conscious substance. Here, knowledge can be said to be analysis. The *ātmā* is of the nature of knowledge, as stated in the *Brahma Sūtra*:

*ātmeti tūpagacchanti grāhayanti ca*

**One should worship the Supreme Ātma with complete attachment as the inner self, because those who are knowledgeable in the truth perceive Him as the very essence of the** ***ātmā*****, and they explain this to their disciples in that manner. (*****Brahma Sūtra*** **4.1.3)**

Prahlāda says in the *Bhāgavata*:

*ātmā nityo’vyayaḥ śuddha ekaḥ kṣetra-jña āśrayaḥ*  
*avikriyaḥ sva-dṛg hetur vyāpako ‘saṅgy anāvṛtaḥ*

**The Supreme Ātmā is eternal, indestructible, pure, singular, the knower of the individual** ***jīvas*****, the supreme shelter, immutable, self-effulgent, the cause of all causes, all-pervading, uninfluenced by matter, and not covered by** ***māyā*****. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **7.7.19)**

Every object has two aspects – its inherent nature (*svarūpa*) and its tendency (*pravṛtti*). Therefore, knowledge is the *ātmā’s* inherent nature, and attachment is its tendency. The only true object of this attachment is the Supreme Lord. However, in the bound state of the *jīva*, this attachment is directed toward inferior objects. Regarding this, *Śrī Viṣṇu Purāṇa* contains the statement of Prahlāda as follows:

*yā prītir avivekānāṁ viṣayeṣv anapāyinī*  
*tvām anusmarataḥ sa me hṛdayān nāpasarpatu*

**The same constant love that those who are indiscriminate feel for the sense objects – may such constant love never leave my heart as I always remember You. (*****Viṣṇu Purāṇa*** **1.20.19)**

In the bound state of the *jīva*, that knowledge does not fully manifest. In the liberated state, the *jñāna-ātmā*–*svarūpa* (the *ātmā* possessing inherent knowledge) exists, as mentioned in the second chapter of the *Gītā*:

*acchedyo’yam adāhyo’yam akledyo’śoṣya eva ca*  
*nityaḥ sarva-gataḥ sthāṇur acalo’yaṁ sanātanaḥ*

**It is indestructible, incombustible, insoluble and cannot be withered. It is eternal, all-pervading, unchanging, immovable and primeval. (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.24)**

However, when the *jīvātmā* is bound, he becomes perverted by *upādhis* (conditional designations) and acquires his present state as a human being. In this state, the mind takes control, and his inherent knowledge becomes hidden, while sense perceptions is mistakenly taken to be knowledge. In reality, there is a significant difference between the knowledge of a liberated *jīva* and that of a bound *jīva*. Knowledge is a pure substance; therefore, it is not bound by space or time. This is why Bhagavān has referred to it as *sarva-gataḥ* (all-pervading). What is called knowledge in this present state is merely a transformational state of knowledge. In reality, it is not actual knowledge. When analysing current knowledge, it is observed that apart from some understanding in relation to the Supreme Truth, everything else that is presently considered to be knowledge is completely based on the senses.

When the senses come into direct contact with an object, the impression of that object, as perceived through the senses, enters the inner chamber of the mental system. Then one of the inner senses carefully retains and preserves that impression. This process can be termed as *dhāraṇā* (mental retention). Subsequently, through *anukalpa* and *vikalpa*, which are the two functions of the internal senses, those mental impressions that have been retained, provide substance for all imaginary experiences. The internal senses, while expanding their dominion over all things, continuously analyses them as good, bad, etc. That analysis can be termed as logic, or rationalism. By specifically analysing this entire process, it can be said to be sensory-based. Being bound by matter, the pure *jñāna-svarūpa* of *the* *ātmā* becomes transformed and attains an adverse condition.

Thus, in the first chapter of the Tenth Canto, Vasudeva says:

*svapne yathā paśyati deham īdṛśaṁ*  
*manorathenābhiniviṣṭa-cetanaḥ*  
*dṛṣṭa-śrutābhyāṁ manasānucintayan*  
*prapadyate tat kim api hy apasmṛtiḥ*  
*yato yato dhāvati daiva-coditaṁ*  
*mano-vikārātmakam āpa pañcasu*  
*guṇeṣu māyā-raciteṣu dehy asau*  
*prapadyamānaḥ saha tena jāyate*

**A person, absorbed in a dream, sees himself with a particular form and contemplates what he is seeing and hearing, accepting that and forgetting his circumstances in the waking state. Wherever the restless mind, impelled by providence, moves, it is shaped by its own transformations and attaches itself to the five elements created by** ***māyā*****. The embodied** ***ātmā*****, identifying with the body fashioned from these elements, is born along with it. (*****Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*** **10.1.41-42)**

The characteristics of pure knowledge are perceived in this way in the *Kaṭhopaniṣad*:

*yadā pañcāvatiṣṭhante jñānāni manasā saha*  
*buddhiś ca na viceṣṭati tām āhuḥ paramāṁ gatīm*

**When the five knowledge-acquiring senses remain peaceful, and the mind and intelligence do not overexert themselves, that state is said to be the highest attainment. (*****Kaṭhopaniṣad*** **2.3.10)**

Although that knowledge transforms into the mind, it does not get destroyed.

*indriyāṇāṁ pṛthag-bhāvaṁ udayāstamayau ca yat*  
*pṛthag utpadyamānānāṁ matvā dhīro na śocati*

**The urges of the various senses come and go. Understanding that the self is different to the sensual body, a sober person does not grieve. (*****Kaṭhopaniṣad*** **2.3.6)**

**Комментарий:**

***tatra rāgasya arthānarthobhaya-mūlaṁ pratipādayati***

Attachment is the root-cause of both *artha* (that which is necessary) and *anartha* (that which is unnecessary).

## Sutra 17

*pare’nuraktiḥ svabhāvikī śreyas-karī ca itareṣv aupādhikī duḥkha-pradā ca*

**Перевод:**

**Attraction to the Supreme is inherent and beneficial, and the opposite is conditional and only gives misery**

**Комментарий:**

***jīvanām ity anuvartate. pare īśvare anuraktiḥ svabhāva-siddhā utkarṣādi-śreyaḥ-sampādayitrī ca bhavati. itareṣu vittāpatya-kalatrādiṣu sā anuraktir aupādhikī saṁsāra-duḥkha-pradā ca bhavatīty arthaḥ. ‘tam eva viditvāti mṛtyum’ eti, ‘ajāhy eko juṣamāno’nuśete’ ityādi-śruteḥ.***

Thus, the *jīvas* follow the Supreme. Attachment to the Supreme Lord is inherent by nature and leads to progress and the attainment of auspiciousness. In contrast, attachment to wealth, family, spouse etc. is conditional and awards one suffering in worldly existence. The *śruti* says, ‘By knowing Him, one overcomes death’ (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 3.8) and  ‘Renouncing everything, the one who is absorbed in the Supreme remains engaged (in contemplation.’ (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 4.5)

**Комментарий. Автор Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:**

Attraction for the Supreme Lord is the inherent tendency of the *jīva*. Just as the attraction of iron is the nature of a magnet, melting is the quality of heat, burning is the potency of fire, *saṅkalpa* and *vikalpa* (accepting and rejecting) are the functions of the mind, and the capability to fulfil their respective purposes is the nature of material objects, similarly, attraction to the Supreme Lord is the *ātmā’s* inherent tendency. In the liberated state, this is the tendency of the *jīva*. It remains pure and fully manifest, but in the bound state, it becomes mutated. The attachment of embodied beings to material objects is a mutation of superior attachment. When that tendency is without material designations, it becomes supreme attachment, but when it acquires such designations, it becomes distorted. Attachment is a singular tendency, yet it acquires different names according to different designations. When there is attachment for money, it is called greed. When attachment arises towards the beauty of a woman, it can be called lechery. When attachment is directed towards the suffering of others, it is termed as compassion. When attachment is directed towards a brother or sister, it is known as affection. When attachment is towards a benefactor, it becomes gratitude. When attachment is directed towards someone who is favourable or supportive, it becomes love. When attachment is designated as hostile, it becomes hatred. In this way, the same inclination manifests in various forms. Its multiplicity is its designation. With a liberated *jīva*, it remains in a state free from designations. It does not simply remain in the same state, but that pure attachment can also infinitely expand – this is its excellence. All these designations have been termed as *dharma* (nature). Therefore, Bhagavān states:

*sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja*  
*ahaṁ tvāṁ sarva-pāpebhyo mokṣayiṣyāmi mā śucaḥ*

**Abandon all types of** ***dharma*** **– come and surrender unto Me alone! Do not fear, for I will surely deliver you from all reactions. (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **18.66)**
